=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given N points (x_1, y_1), (x_2, y_2), \dots, (x_N, y_N) on a two-dimensional plane, and a non-negative integer D.
Find the number of integer pairs (x, y) such that \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N (|x-x_i|+|y-y_i|) \leq D.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N D
x_1 y_1
x_2 y_2
\vdots
x_N y_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq D \leq 10^6
- -10^6 \leq x_i, y_i \leq 10^6
- (x_i, y_i) \neq (x_j, y_j) for i \neq j.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 3
0 0
1 0

Sample Output 1

8

The following figure visualizes the input and the answer for Sample 1. The blue points represent the input. The blue and red points, eight in total, satisfy the condition in the statement.

Sample Input 2

2 0
0 0
2 0

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

6 100
9 -6
10 -1
2 10
-1 7
-7 5
-1 -4

Sample Output 3

419

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, find_interval function:

def get_interval(arr, pref, total, n, D, med):
    # returns (L,R) inclusive of integer x with F(x)<=D, assuming F(med)<=D
    lo = min(arr) - D - 1
    hi = med
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        k = bisect_right(arr, mid)
        f = (2*k - n)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
        if f <= D:
            hi = mid
        else:
            lo = mid
    L = hi
    lo = med
    hi = max(arr) + D + 1
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        k = bisect_right(arr, mid)
        f = (2*k - n)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
        if f <= D:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    R = lo
    return L,R

Need min(arr), max(arr) each call. We can precompute min_x,max_x,min_y,max_y to avoid O(N) repeated. But O(N) fine. We'll precompute.

Now, count_y array computation:

def build_count(arr, pref, total, n, D):
    med = arr[n//2]
    if F(med) > D: return None
    L,R = get_interval(...)
    freq = [0]*(D+1)
    # iterate y from L to R
    # To compute F_y efficiently, we can maintain k? Since y increases by 1, k = bisect_right(arr, y) changes. We can use bisect_right each time. But 2e6 calls okay. However we can optimize by maintaining pointer because y range maybe large but sorted arr. Let's think.
    for y in range(L, R+1):
        k = bisect_right(arr, y)
        f = (2*k - n)*y + total - 2*pref[k]
        freq[f] += 1
    # prefix
    for t in range(1, D+1):
        freq[t] += freq[t-1]
    return freq

Need if D=0, freq length 1, prefix loop range(1,1) none. Good.

Potential issue: y interval length could be 0? If F(med)<=D, at least med. L<=R. Good.

Now, answer loop:

ans=0
for x in range(Lx, Rx+1):
    k = bisect_right(xs, x)
    fx = (2*k - N)*x + total_x - 2*prefix_x[k]
    T = D - fx
    ans += count_y[T]
print(ans)

import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, D = map(int, input().split())
    xs = [0]*N
    ys = [0]*N
    for i in range(N):
        x,y = map(int, input().split())
        xs[i]=x; ys[i]=y
    xs.sort(); ys.sort()
    pref_x=[0]*(N+1)
    for i,x in enumerate(xs):
        pref_x[i+1]=pref_x[i]+x
    pref_y similarly.
    total_x=pref_x[N]; total_y=pref_y[N]
    min_x=xs[0]; max_x=xs[-1]; min_y=ys[0]; max_y=ys[-1]
    med_x=xs[N//2]; med_y=ys[N//2]
    # define F? To avoid function overhead maybe define helper with closure.
    def value(arr, pref, total, x):
        k=bisect_right(arr, x)
        return (2*k - N)*x + total - 2*pref[k]
    if value(xs,pref_x,total_x,med_x) > D or value(ys,pref_y,total_y,med_y) > D:
        print(0); return
    def get_interval(arr, pref, total, med, minv, maxv):
        lo = minv - D - 1
        hi = med
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi)//2
            k = bisect_right(arr, mid)
            f = (2*k - N)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid
        L = hi
        lo = med
        hi = maxv + D + 1
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi)//2
            k = bisect_right(arr, mid)
            f = (2*k - N)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        R = lo
        return L,R
    Lx,Rx = get_interval(xs,pref_x,total_x,med_x,min_x,max_x)
    Ly,Ry = get_interval(ys,pref_y,total_y,med_y,min_y,max_y)
    freq=[0]*(D+1)
    # iterate y
    for y in range(Ly, Ry+1):
        k = bisect_right(ys, y)
        f = (2*k - N)*y + total_y - 2*pref_y[k]
        freq[f] += 1
    for t in range(1, D+1):
        freq[t] += freq[t-1]
    ans=0
    for x in range(Lx, Rx+1):
        k = bisect_right(xs, x)
        fx = (2*k - N)*x + total_x - 2*pref_x[k]
        ans += freq[D - fx]
    print(ans)

Let's maybe derive a formal proof to ensure no hidden flaw.

Define F(t)=Σ|t-a_i|. For any two integers u,v, F(u)+F(v) = Σ(|u-a_i|+|v-a_i|) ≥ Σ|u-v| = N|u-v|. Therefore if F(u)≤D and F(v)≤D, then |u-v|≤2D/N. Hence the set S_x={x: F_x(x)≤D} is contained in an interval of length at most 2D/N, and similarly S_y. Since F is convex, S_x and S_y are intervals. We find their endpoints by binary search using monotonicity on each side of a median. Then count y by frequency.

The only possible issue: The set S_x is contained in interval of length ≤2D/N, but our binary search lo/hi range is much larger. We find exact endpoints. Good.

Let's maybe refine code for speed and clarity.

Potential speed improvements:
- Bind `bisect_right` to local `br`.
- Bind lists to local variables in loops.
- In get_interval, use local `br`, `arr`, `pref`, `total`, `n`, `D`.
- In count_y loop, local variables.
- In answer loop, local variables.

Let's write:

from bisect import bisect_right
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N_D = input().split()
    if not N_D: return
    N = int(N_D[0]); D = int(N_D[1])
    xs=[0]*N; ys=[0]*N
    for i in range(N):
        x_str = input().split()
        xs[i]=int(x_str[0]); ys[i]=int(x_str[1])
    xs.sort(); ys.sort()
    pref_x=[0]*(N+1)
    s=0
    for i,x in enumerate(xs):
        s += x
        pref_x[i+1]=s
    total_x=s
    pref_y=[0]*(N+1)
    s=0
    for i,y in enumerate(ys):
        s += y
        pref_y[i+1]=s
    total_y=s

    br = bisect_right
    n=N
    def f_value(arr, pref, total, v):
        k = br(arr, v)
        return (2*k - n)*v + total - 2*pref[k]

    med_x = xs[n//2]
    med_y = ys[n//2]
    if f_value(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x) > D or f_value(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y) > D:
        print(0)
        return

    def get_interval(arr, pref, total, med, minv, maxv):
        lo = minv - D - 1
        hi = med
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2*k - n)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid
        L = hi

        lo = med
        hi = maxv + D + 1
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2*k - n)*mid + total - 2*pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        R = lo
        return L, R

    Lx, Rx = get_interval(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x, xs[0], xs[-1])
    Ly, Ry = get_interval(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y, ys[0], ys[-1])

    freq = [0]*(D+1)
    # local bindings
    arr_y = ys; pref_y_local = pref_y; total_y_local = total_y
    for y in range(Ly, Ry+1):
        k = br(arr_y, y)
        f = (2*k - n)*y + total_y_local - 2*pref_y_local[k]
        freq[f] += 1

    for t in range(1, D+1):
        freq[t] += freq[t-1]

    ans = 0
    arr_x = xs; pref_x_local = pref_x; total_x_local = total_x
    for x in range(Lx, Rx+1):
        k = br(arr_x, x)
        fx = (2*k - n)*x + total_x_local - 2*pref_x_local[k]
        ans += freq[D - fx]

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    k += 1

If Ly < point, k increments at point, then k=1, for subsequent y, `k < n` false, so condition short-circuits and is cheap. If Ly > point, k initialized to1, condition false each time. So pointer could be faster. But code complexity slightly more. We can keep bisect for simplicity. The final answer should be robust.

Let's write final code carefully:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, D = map(int, input().split())
    xs = [0] * N
    ys = [0] * N
    for i in range(N):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        xs[i] = x
        ys[i] = y

    xs.sort()
    ys.sort()

    pref_x = [0] * (N + 1)
    s = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(xs):
        s += v
        pref_x[i + 1] = s
    total_x = s

    pref_y = [0] * (N + 1)
    s = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(ys):
        s += v
        pref_y[i + 1] = s
    total_y = s

    br = bisect_right
    n = N

    def value(arr, pref, total, v):
        k = br(arr, v)
        return (2 * k - n) * v + total - 2 * pref[k]

    med_x = xs[n // 2]
    med_y = ys[n // 2]

    if value(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x) > D or value(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y) > D:
        print(0)
        return

    def get_interval(arr, pref, total, med, minv, maxv):
        lo = minv - D - 1
        hi = med
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2 * k - n) * mid + total - 2 * pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid
        L = hi

        lo = med
        hi = maxv + D + 1
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2 * k - n) * mid + total - 2 * pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        R = lo
        return L, R

    Lx, Rx = get_interval(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x, xs[0], xs[-1])
    Ly, Ry = get_interval(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y, ys[0], ys[-1])

    freq = [0] * (D + 1)

    k = br(ys, Ly)
    for y in range(Ly, Ry + 1):
        while k < n and ys[k] <= y:
            k += 1
        f = (2 * k - n) * y + total_y - 2 * pref_y[k]
        freq[f] += 1

    for t in range(1, D + 1):
        freq[t] += freq[t - 1]

    ans = 0
    k = br(xs, Lx)
    for x in range(Lx, Rx + 1):
        while k < n and xs[k] <= x:
            k += 1
        fx = (2 * k - n) * x + total_x - 2 * pref_x[k]
        ans += freq[D - fx]

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's maybe run through sample 1 with pointer method:
xs=[0,1], ys=[0,0], N=2,D=3.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `ans` loop uses `k` variable and while, but if `x` interval length is large and `xs` has many duplicates, while increments through duplicates at the same x. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `k` initialized with `br(xs, Lx)` and if Lx is exactly a data point, br includes it. Then while at x=Lx will check the next point after those <=Lx, which is >Lx, so false. So k remains correct. If we had initialized k with bisect_left, would need while, but we use br. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `br(ys, Ly)` and Ly is a data point, includes duplicates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `while k < n and ys[k] <= y:` and y increments by 1, but if there are multiple points with same y, while increments all when y reaches that value. Since br(Ly) already includes points <=Ly, if Ly is less than that value, k will be before them, and while increments all at that y. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `ans` loop uses `k` variable and while, but if `x` interval length is large and `xs` has many duplicates, while increments through duplicates at the same x. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `k` initialized with `br(xs, Lx)` and if Lx is exactly a data point, br includes it. Then while at x=Lx will check the next point after those <=Lx, which is >Lx, so false. So k remains correct. If we had initialized k with bisect_left, would need while, but we use br. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `br(ys, Ly)` and Ly is a data point, includes duplicates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `while k < n and ys[k] <= y:` and y increments by 1, but if there are multiple points with same y, while increments all when y reaches that value. Since br(Ly) already includes points <=Ly, if Ly is less than that value, k will be before them, and while increments all at that y. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `ans` loop uses `k` variable and while, but if `x` interval length is large and `xs` has many duplicates, while increments through duplicates at the same x. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `k` initialized with `br(xs, Lx)` and if Lx is exactly a data point, br includes it. Then while at x=Lx will check the next point after those <=Lx, which is >Lx, so false. So k remains correct. If we had initialized k with bisect_left, would need while, but we use br. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with if `br(ys, Ly)` and Ly is a data point, includes duplicates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `while k < n and ys[k] <= y:` and y increments by 1, but if there are multiple points with same y, while increments all when y reaches that value. Since br(Ly) already includes points <=Ly, if Ly is less than that value, k will be before them, and while increments all at that y. Good.

Since br(Ly) already includes points <=Ly, if Ly is less than that value, k will be before them, and while increments all at that y. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if `while k < n and ys[k]
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right


def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, D = map(int, input().split())

    xs = [0] * N
    ys = [0] * N
    for i in range(N):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        xs[i] = x
        ys[i] = y

    xs.sort()
    ys.sort()

    pref_x = [0] * (N + 1)
    s = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(xs):
        s += v
        pref_x[i + 1] = s
    total_x = s

    pref_y = [0] * (N + 1)
    s = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(ys):
        s += v
        pref_y[i + 1] = s
    total_y = s

    br = bisect_right
    n = N

    def value(arr, pref, total, v):
        k = br(arr, v)
        return (2 * k - n) * v + total - 2 * pref[k]

    med_x = xs[n // 2]
    med_y = ys[n // 2]

    if value(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x) > D or value(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y) > D:
        print(0)
        return

    def get_interval(arr, pref, total, med, minv, maxv):
        lo = minv - D - 1
        hi = med
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2 * k - n) * mid + total - 2 * pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid
        L = hi

        lo = med
        hi = maxv + D + 1
        while hi - lo > 1:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            k = br(arr, mid)
            f = (2 * k - n) * mid + total - 2 * pref[k]
            if f <= D:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        R = lo

        return L, R

    Lx, Rx = get_interval(xs, pref_x, total_x, med_x, xs[0], xs[-1])
    Ly, Ry = get_interval(ys, pref_y, total_y, med_y, ys[0], ys[-1])

    freq = [0] * (D + 1)

    k = br(ys, Ly)
    for y in range(Ly, Ry + 1):
        while k < n and ys[k] <= y:
            k += 1
        f = (2 * k - n) * y + total_y - 2 * pref_y[k]
        freq[f] += 1

    for t in range(1, D + 1):
        freq[t] += freq[t - 1]

    ans = 0
    k = br(xs, Lx)
    for x in range(Lx, Rx + 1):
        while k < n and xs[k] <= x:
            k += 1
        fx = (2 * k - n) * x + total_x - 2 * pref_x[k]
        ans += freq[D - fx]

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
